# Style study

This document gives the style parameters for *G-Type Star*.
The parameters come from the music of the band Sungazer and of five circles of artists around it.
The composition code uses these parameters as its rules.

## Method

The study has three types of data. Each row of a table has a code for its type.

| Code | Type | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| M | Measured | Code measured audio recordings, score data, or performance data. |
| R | Read | The study team read the source. The column "Source" gives the key of the source. The list of sources is at the end of this document. |
| K | Reference knowledge | The row is standard knowledge, or an estimate. The study team did not read a source for the row. Check the row before you use it for a critical decision. |

The study measured six tracks of the album *Against the Fall of Night* (Sungazer, 2024), which the owner of the project has.
No open score data are available for this music.
Thus the study got its data from the audio:

1. A program divided each track into four stems: drums, bass, other instruments, and voice.
2. Code found the fastest regular pulse of each track, and the strokes of the kick drum, the snare drum, and the cymbals.
3. Code found the notes of the bass and of the other instruments.
4. Code measured the loudness, the tone balance, and the stereo width of each track.

The results are approximations. The study uses them for statistics, not as a score.
The recordings, the stems, and the notes are not in the repository. Only numbers that describe the style are in the repository.

The sourced tables come from seven research tasks (15 November 2026): Sungazer; large-ensemble fusion (Snarky Puppy); live-electronic fusion; progressive and djent fusion; cosmic jazz; the beat scene, IDM, and dubstep; and the science of human feel with the statistics of fast lines.
Two of these tasks also measured open data: the Groove MIDI Dataset (drummers) and the Weimar Jazz Database (solos).

## Main results

1. The reference tracks are loud and dense: -9.6 to -12.7 LUFS, and 53 to 77 percent of the time at the full level.
   The contrast comes from quiet parts and from short stops of the full band (0.6 to 2.3 stops in a minute).
2. The bars of the drums are almost never the same two times.
   In five of six tracks, a bar of kick and snare is the same as the bar before it in 0 to 15 percent of the bars.
3. The drummer is exact, but not a machine: the strokes of one drum have a spread of 8 to 12 ms against the pulse.
4. The metres are cycles of a fast pulse: 15 pulses (Whalefall), 12 (Skrontch), 10 (Whisky and Mes), 5 and 25 (Paydushko Horo).
   A track keeps its pulse and changes the grouping: Whalefall has 5 groups of 3 pulses, then 3 groups of 5.
   The sources give the same fact: a change of tempo by the ratio 5 to 3.
5. The bass is low and short: one third to one half of its notes are below A1, and the median note has 0.13 to 0.16 seconds.
6. The mix has the drums and the other instruments at the same loudness (4.2 LU below the mix), and the bass 2.7 LU below them.
7. The model track "Cool 7" has seven quarter notes in a bar, as four long pulses and one short pulse.
   Half way through its melody, each long pulse gets five notes and the short pulse gets three.
8. The melodies of the band are slow and in the scale, above chords that are complex: "Drunk" has 3.27 beats for each melody note.
9. Published measurements give the size of human timing: a soloist is behind the beat by about 9 percent of a beat on the beats and on the grid between them; a ghost note has 0.28 of the key velocity of a backbeat.
10. Fast lines of fusion players have 8.2 notes in a second (median) and 12.2 (90th percentile). Sixteenth notes at the main tempo of the work have 11.7.

## Part 1: Measured tables

Code made all tables of this part. The directory [`research/measured`](../research/measured) has the data files.

### M1. Reference tracks: loudness and stereo width

The loudness unit is the LUFS (ITU-R BS.1770). The loudness range is the difference between the loud and the soft passages (EBU Tech 3342). The short-term values are relative to the integrated loudness. The side level is the level of the difference of the two channels, relative to their sum.

| Recording | Minutes | Integrated loudness lufs | Loudness range lu | Short term 10th percentile lu | Short term median lu | Short term 95th percentile lu | Peak db | Crest factor db | Left right correlation | Side level db |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Sungazer, Whalefall | 5.77 | -9.9 | 8.2 | -5.8 | -0.1 | 2.4 | -0.1 | 12.5 | 0.73 | -8.1 |
| Sungazer, Skrontch | 4.91 | -11.0 | 4.6 | -2.6 | -0.7 | 1.9 | 0.2 | 13.4 | 0.75 | -8.5 |
| Sungazer, Hot Saturn | 3.42 | -10.8 | 5.0 | -2.8 | 0.1 | 2.2 | 0.0 | 12.6 | 0.80 | -9.6 |
| Sungazer, Oeteldonk | 5.26 | -10.2 | 12.6 | -11.1 | -0.1 | 1.5 | 0.1 | 12.6 | 0.71 | -7.7 |
| Sungazer, Whisky and Mes | 5.31 | -9.6 | 5.7 | -3.0 | -0.5 | 2.7 | 0.0 | 11.7 | 0.77 | -8.8 |
| Sungazer, Paydushko Horo | 5.28 | -12.7 | 10.7 | -8.3 | 0.3 | 2.3 | 0.0 | 14.6 | 0.80 | -9.4 |

Data file: [`research/measured/reference_recordings.csv`](../research/measured/reference_recordings.csv)

### M3. Reference tracks: pulse, cycles, strokes, and repetition

The pulse is the fastest regular note value of a track. A cycle is the number of pulses after which the pattern of the snare drum (or of the kick drum) comes again; the percentages are shares of the windows of 192 pulses. The last columns show how frequently a bar of kick and snare (a cycle of the main length) is the same as the bar before it, or as one of the 8 bars before it.

| Recording | Pulse ms | Pulses per second | Cycle lengths in pulses | Main cycle pulses | Main cycle seconds | Kick strokes per second | Snare strokes per second | Cymbal strokes per second | Kick share at half a pulse | Bars same as the bar before | Bars same as one of 8 bars before | Bars |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Sungazer, Whalefall | 146.3 | 6.835 | 15 (76%); 6 (10%); 30 (5%) | 15 | 2.194 | 2.347 | 0.836 | 2.868 | 0.027 | 0.059 | 0.104 | 135 |
| Sungazer, Skrontch | 111.1 | 9.001 | 12 (48%); 24 (44%); 36 (8%) | 12 | 1.333 | 1.727 | 1.324 | 5.374 | 0.019 | 0.072 | 0.335 | 194 |
| Sungazer, Hot Saturn | 96.3 | 10.384 | 32 (30%); 8 (25%); 12 (15%) | 32 | 3.082 | 2.66 | 0.729 | 4.312 | 0.06 | 0 | 0 | 63 |
| Sungazer, Oeteldonk | 208.4 | 4.798 | 4 (45%); 18 (18%); 6 (18%) | 4 | 0.834 | 1.871 | 1.584 | 3.838 | 0.004 | 0.359 | 0.752 | 270 |
| Sungazer, Whisky and Mes | 157.8 | 6.337 | 10 (74%); 20 (16%); 5 (11%) | 10 | 1.578 | 2.433 | 0.892 | 4.556 | 0.008 | 0.145 | 0.279 | 179 |
| Sungazer, Paydushko Horo | 111 | 9.009 | 25 (35%); 5 (19%); 10 (8%) | 25 | 2.775 | 1.376 | 0.816 | 3.876 | 0.081 | 0.012 | 0.06 | 84 |

| Sungazer | Sungazer | Form by change of subdivision | Sections are set apart by the subdivision, not by the tempo. Main groove: 11 notes per beat. Bridge: the 11-pattern is expanded and gets a hemiola. Breakdown: 7 notes per beat on the same pulse. The pulse and the click stay the same. | Section types named by Neely: groove, bridge, breakdown. 11 to 7 notes per beat = 0.64 of the note rate (computed) | Against the Fall of Night (title track) | neely-44 | R |
| Sungazer | Sungazer | Form in two halves with one melody | The melody is written to work in both feels. First half: the long pulses feel like the quarter notes of a 4/4 bar. Second half: the same cycle gets a quintuplet subdivision and feels like 7/4. The change comes half way through the melody. | Change point = middle of the melody. Audience dance: 2 steps right, 2 steps left; steps on counts 1 and 3 of 4 before the change, on counts 1 and 4 of 7 after it | Cool 7 | neely-cool7 | R |

At the drop the percussion stops, often to silence, and then starts again with more force and a dominant sub-bass. | The bass usually drops after bar 16 or bar 32 | Genre practice | wiki-dubstep | R |
| Beat scene, IDM, dubstep | Dubstep | Form in blocks of 8 bars | A tutorial for the UK 140 style plans the track in blocks of 8 bars. | Intro 8 bars; build 8 bars; drop parts 1A, 1B and 1C of 8 bars each. At 140 BPM: 8 bars = 13.7 s; 16 bars = 27.4 s (computed) | Tutorial example | edmprod-dubstep | R |
| Beat scene, IDM, dubstep | Dubstep | Bar budget for a passage of 30 seconds | At 140 BPM one bar of 4/4 lasts 1.714 s. | 17 bars = 29.1 s; 16 bars = 27.4 s. Possible plans: 8-bar build + 8-bar drop; or 4-bar build + 12-bar drop; or a 16-bar drop with the build before the 30 seconds. At 150 BPM: 18 bars = 28.8 s. Computed values; the plans are an estimate | New work | reference | K |
| Beat scene, IDM, dubstep | Dubstep | Build-up and the gap before the drop | The build-up repeats one figure, makes the drums faster and makes the volume grow. Many tracks have a break of half a bar or one bar before the drop. A vocal sample or a reverb tail fills the break. | The snare roll gets faster; synth swells rise; a high-pass filter on the full mix goes up; the energy below 100 Hz is kept for the drop | Genre practice | wiki-drop; edmprod-buildups | R |
| Beat scene, IDM, dubstep | Dubstep | Snare roll and kick roll in the build | The roll doubles its rate in steps. Automation can make the pitch of the snare rise. | Roll of almost 8 bars: 4 bars with a snare on each beat, then eighth notes, then faster values. Kick build: double the number of kicks after 4 bars and double it again after 2 more bars | Tutorial examples | edmprod-buildups; edmprod-dubstep | R |
| Beat scene, IDM, dubstep | Skrillex | Vocal tag before the drop | A short shouted voice sample comes immediately before the drop. | Sample of 4 words; drop at about 0:40 = about bar 23 to 24 at 140 BPM (computed) | Scary Monsters and Nice Sprites | wiki-scary-monsters | R |

### S5. Rhythm and metre

| Circle | Artist or study | Item | Content | Values | Where | Source | Code |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Sungazer | Shawn Crowder (drums) | Hypertuplets ('high resolution rhythms') | Crowder's name for grooves that divide each beat of a slow 4/4 into more than 10 equal notes. The 11-tuplet is the mildest one. Quintuplet and septuplet grooves are the usual tuplet grooves of the band. | 11, 13, 15, 17 or 19 notes per beat. Examples of the pulse: 33 BPM and 58 BPM | Against the Fall of Night; Threshold; other tunes of the 2021 album | neely-44; laughingsquid-extreme | R |
| Sungazer | Sungazer | Groups inside the 11-tuplet | The 11 notes are grouped as in the Bulgarian dances gankino horo and kopanitsa, and as in a well-known drumline warm-up (Crowder likes drum corps). Neely finds the grouping 3+3+3+2 less natural. | 2+2+3+2+2 = quick, quick, slow, quick, quick | Against the Fall of Night (title track) | neely-44; wiki-kopanitsa | R |
| Sungazer | Sungazer | Hypertuplet aksak (the phantom three) | The groups differ by less than 100 ms. Thus listeners smooth 2+2+3+2+2 into three almost equal beats (4+3+4). They hear and move to a fast 3/4, not to the slow 4/4. Neely jokes that everything 'evolves to three'. | 58 BPM pulse: felt quarter = 174 BPM, beat ratio 4:3:4. 33 BPM pulse with 6+6+7: felt 3/4 near 99 BPM, beats of 574, 574 and 670 ms (computed) | Against the Fall of Night; Threshold | neely-44 | R |
| Sungazer | Sungazer | Written metre and counted metre | The band counts off and sets the click in 4/4 on the slow pulse. The parts are written in an odd metre with the tuplet number on top, one bar for each beat, because tuplets with syncopation are hard to read. | One bar of 11/16 = one beat of the slow 4/4 = 5.5 eighth notes | Against the Fall of Night (title track) | neely-44 | R |
| Sungazer | Sungazer | Hemiola inside a tuplet grid | In the bridge an accent on each third 11-tuplet makes a three-feel against the 11. | Accent on notes 1, 4, 7, 10 and so on. The accent pattern and the 11-cycle meet again after 33 notes = 3 beats (computed) | Against the Fall of Night (title track), bridge | neely-44 | R |
| Sungazer | Sungazer | Change of subdivision on one pulse (7 against 11) | The band changes the number of notes per beat and keeps the pulse. From 11 to 7 sounds like a slower tempo; back to 11 sounds like a faster tempo. In the odd-metre parts this is written as a 7:11 polyrhythm. | At 58 BPM: 11 per beat = 10.6 notes per second; 7 per beat = 6.8 notes per second (computed) | Against the Fall of Night (title track), breakdown | neely-44 | R |
| Sungazer | Sungazer | Quintuplet swing | The beat is divided in 5 and the notes come on parts 1 and 4. The feel is between straight and triplet swing: 'almost triplets, but not'. Crowder likes this feel. | Long to short = 3:2 (60 percent). Compare: straight 50 percent; septuplet swing 57.1; triplet swing 66.7. Jazz swing ratios in general: 1:1 to 3:1 | Sequence Start; The Dark (written in quintuplets) | pudding-groove; hein-swing; neely-covers; tvdb-seqstart; wiki-swing | R |
| Sungazer | Sungazer | Septuplet swing | The beat is divided in 7 as 4+3. Neely used septuplets to get near a 'drunken' backbeat in a dubstep setting. | Long to short = 4:3 (57.1 percent) | Dream of Mahjong | hein-swing; tvdb-mahjong; cytoid-mahjong | R |
| Sungazer | Reference | The stock 5/4 pattern that Ether avoids | The pattern that most pieces in 5/4 use, as in Take Five. | 3+3+2+2 eighth notes (dotted quarter, dotted quarter, quarter, quarter). This value was not confirmed in a source | General practice | reference | K |
| Sungazer | Sungazer | Clave of long and short pulses | A groove is made from uneven pulses in a fixed order. The backbeat is on long pulses 2 and 4, so the long pulses sound like the beats of a 4/4 bar and the short pulse like a small extra beat. | Long = dotted quarter (3 eighths). Short = quarter (2 eighths). Ratio 3:2. Pattern long, long, long, long, short = 14 eighths | Cool 7 | neely-cool7 | R |
| Sungazer | Sungazer | Pulse morph (new subdivision of uneven pulses) | Crowder's idea: each long pulse gets 5 notes and the short pulse gets 3. The two note speeds are not the same. The band stretched the notes so that the long and short pulses keep their exact lengths. The other choice (even notes, pulse lengths only nearly right) sounds more organic but does not fit a laptop with layers on top. | 23 notes per cycle (5+5+5+5+3). Note length 0.600 eighth in the long pulses and 0.667 eighth in the short pulse: 11 percent longer (computed) | Cool 7, second half | neely-cool7; bluesuns-atfon | R |
| Sungazer | Reference | Note spacing in Cool 7 (estimate) | Estimate from the database tempo of 183 BPM for the quarter note. The fast notes of the second half are at the limit of rhythm perception. | Estimate: eighth = 164 ms; long pulse = 492 ms (122 BPM); cycle = 2.30 s; quintuplet note = 98 ms; triplet note of the short pulse = 109 ms | Cool 7 | reference | K |
| Sungazer | Sungazer | Metric modulation by 5:3 | Each fifth eighth-note triplet becomes the new beat: a quarter note tied to a quarter-note triplet equals the new quarter note. The way back uses three quintuplets of the slow tempo. A reviewer describes the same device: the piece switches between 5 beats of triplets and 3 beats of quintuplets. | 136 BPM to 82 BPM. New tempo = old tempo x 3/5. 5 beats x 3 notes = 3 beats x 5 notes = 15 notes. General formula: new tempo / old tempo = number of pivot notes in the new bar / number in the old bar | Whalefall | waterson-whalefall; bluesuns-atfon; wiki-metricmod | R |
| Sungazer | Shawn Crowder (drums) | Drum devices in five | A drum feature goes from quintuplets to sextuplets to septuplets; the quintuplet licks are felt as 2+3 or 3+2. In 5/4 the drums play kick, snare, snare, so a 3/4 waltz feel goes across the bar. Also: a kick on all five beats ('five-on-the-floor'); a five-note pattern in triplets on the cymbal, phrased 2+3; off-beat triplets in the first groove. | 5, 6, 7 notes per beat at 82 BPM = 6.8, 8.2, 9.6 notes per second (computed). 3-beat cell against a 5-beat bar: the two meet after 15 beats (computed) | Whalefall | waterson-whalefall | R |
| Sungazer | Sungazer | Odd metres from Balkan dance | Balkan groupings are a constant source. Titles and metres come from dances. | Bird on the Wing: 9/8 as 2+3+2+2. | Live-electronic fusion | Genre reference | Drum and bass groove on a kit | Two-bar patterns. Constant eighth notes on the hi-hat. Kick and snare change. Many ghost notes. | Target 160 to 180 BPM; practice from 50 BPM. Hi-hat at the target: 5.3 to 6.0 notes per second (computed) | Lesson | drumninja-dnb | R |
| Live-electronic fusion | Genre reference | Amen break | The most used break of jungle and drum and bass. | 4 bars in 7 seconds (137 BPM, computed; a linked tutorial gives about 136). Bars 1 and 2 are the same. Bar 3 has a delayed snare. Bar 4 has an empty first beat, then syncopation and an early crash. Producers change the pitch and speed and re-order the parts | "Amen, Brother" (1969) | wiki-amen; wiki-dnb | R |
| Live-electronic fusion | Genre reference | Break change, half-time, house and footwork patterns | Rules of the source styles. | Drum and bass: change between two breaks after each bar; half-time beat at the same tempo. House: kick on beats 1-2-3-4, clap on 2 and 4, hi-hat off the beat. Footwork: kicks skip beats; full-time and half-time sections alternate | Source styles | wiki-dnb; wiki-house; wiki-footwork | R |
| Live-electronic fusion | DOMi & JD Beck | JD Beck: half-time feel on a fast pulse | A "highly embellished half time feel". | Pulse about 176 BPM. Right hand: running eighth notes (5.9 per second). Left hand: 16th-note texture between them (11.7 per second together). Many paradiddle inversions. Busy bass drum with a fixed pattern | Zildjian video from 1:47 | bishop-beck-2023 | R |
| Live-electronic fusion | DOMi & JD Beck | Metres and vamp lengths | Odd metres under an easy surface. | SMiLE: 4/4, 7/4 and "5/5" (as printed). TAKE A CHANCE: 7/4. TWO SHRiMPS: 9/4. Extra beats in bridges. Live vamps: 18 beats (3 bars of 6/4), then 8 bars of 4/4, then 3/4 at the same tempo | NOT TiGHT; Zildjian video | quietus-not-tight; bluenote-not-tight; bishop-beck-2023 | R |
| Live-electronic fusion | DOMi & JD Beck | JD Beck: two tempos together | Any tempo fits on any other tempo. They meet at one beat. | His example: a hip-hop beat at 92 BPM with a shuffle at 130 BPM on top (ratio 1.41) | Interview 2019 | md-beck-2019 | R |
| Live-electronic fusion | DOMi & JD Beck | JD Beck: vocabulary | Single strokes and ghost notes with a clear pulse. Phrases go over the bar line. | Practice: flam taps, paradiddles, singles, doubles. Backbeat on 2 and 4 in most of his videos. Groupings and wide dynamics from gospel drumming, at fast tempos | 2019 | md-beck-2019; bonedo-beck | R |
| Live-electronic fusion | Louis Cole | One-handed 16th notes | One hand plays straight 16th notes through a full fast song. | Practice: a fast song of 3 to 4 minutes with no tension. At 138 BPM this is 9.2 notes per second (computed). Also groups of 5 or 6 notes with one hand; the stick bounces and the fingers keep time | Interview 2019 | md-cole-2019; songbpm-knower | R |
| Live-electronic fusion | Louis Cole | Open-handed funk | Open-handed playing with ghost notes in the manner of the James Brown drummers. | Models: "Funky Drummer" and the four-on-the-floor funk beat of "Talking Loud and Saying Nothing". The band enters at 0:34 with the main pattern | Overtime (Live Band sesh) | md-cole-2019 | R |
| Live-electronic fusion | Louis Cole | Even level and tight time | Each backbeat and each ghost note has the same level. For fast funk the time is as tight as a metronome. | He practises time, not beats. About 4 hours of drums each day | 2019 | md-cole-2019; lobf-cole-2019 | R |
| Live-electronic fusion | Mark Guiliana | D.R.O.P. method | Four tools: Dynamics, Rate, Orchestration, Phrasing. | "The Loop": a one-bar pattern that mixes divisions in two and in three. Orchestration: 7 combinations of instruments on a small kit. Phrasing: a paradiddle moved across triplets. Book of 88 pages | Exploring Your Creativity on the Drumset (2016) | bonedo-guiliana-book; guiliana-teaching | R |
| Live-electronic fusion | Mark Guiliana | Loop played by hand | The drummer implies a loop but plays it live. A played loop is not the same each time. | He copies the "ignorance and relentless character" of the drum machine. Sources: Squarepusher (1996), Aphex Twin (2001), Photek (1998), Plug (1996). He could not copy them and kept the space in between | 2015 to 2019 | popmatters-guiliana-2019; wbgo-guiliana-2018; irishtimes-guiliana-2015 | R |
| Live-electronic fusion | Tennyson | Fast random arpeggio | An arpeggiator in random mode at a rate of 1/64. | At 129 BPM this is 34 notes per second (computed): faster than a player | "Nine Lives" | ableton-tennyson-2022; songbpm-tennyson | R |
| Live-electronic fusion | All (estimate) | Notes per second at 174 BPM | Arithmetic table for the centre tempo of drum and bass. | Eighths 5.8; 16ths 11.6; quintuplets 14.5; sextuplets 17.4; septuplets 20.3; 32nds 23.2 notes per second (computed; which of these rates the players use is an estimate) |  | reference | K |
| Live-electronic fusion | All (estimate) | Two-step drum and bass bar | The standard bar on a grid of 16 steps. | Kick on steps 1 and 11. Snare on steps 5 and 13. Hi-hat on the eighths. Ghost snares on some of steps 8, 10, 15, 16 (estimate) |  | reference | K |
| Live-electronic fusion | All (estimate) | Swing between grid points | "Drunk" hip-hop feel as quintuplet or septuplet swing. | Straight 50 %; septuplet swing (4+3) 57 %; quintuplet swing (3+2) 60 %; triplet 67 %. No source for these artists was read (estimate) |  | reference | K |
| Live-electronic fusion | All (estimate) | Limits of the hands | Speeds that expert drummers hold for long passages. | Two hands in single strokes: 13 to 15 notes per second. One hand: 8 to 10 notes per second. Short bursts: to 20 per second (estimate) |  | reference | K |
| Progressive and djent fusion | Meshuggah | The cycle method | A riff of an odd length repeats over a plain 4/4 pulse in the drums. The riff moves out of line with the bar. After 4, 8 or 16 bars the riff is cut or changed, and all parts start again together. The wait for this meeting point makes the tension; harmony and melody do not need to change (said of Hamasyan's use of the same method). | Cycle = 64, 128 or 256 sixteenth notes (4, 8 or 16 bars). Hagström: 'Everything we do is based around a 4/4 core'. Some riffs come back into line only after 16 bars |  | wiki-meshuggah; lucas-2018; rose-2013; pg-hagstrom-koloss; amarii-2025 | R |
| Progressive and djent fusion | Meshuggah | 'New Millennium Cyanide Christ': 23 and 9 | Two riffs of the song show two ways to close the cycle: cut the last statement, or add one note. | Opening riff: pattern of 23 sixteenths; 5 full statements (115) + a fragment of 13 = 128 = 8 bars of 4/4; a crash cymbal marks the restart. Second riff: 9 sixteenths x 7 = 63, + 1 added sixteenth = 64 = 4 bars; crash on each beat, snare on beat 3, kick doubles the guitars | New Millennium Cyanide Christ (Chaosphere) | rose-2013 | R |
| Progressive and djent fusion | Meshuggah | 'Stengah' riffs D and E: 22 and 10 | Riff D shortens its last statement. Riff E puts its extra notes in the middle of the cycle, so the end joins the start with no seam. | Riff D: 22 sixteenths grouped 3+4+3+3+4+5; two statements + one of 20 (last group 3) = 64 = 4 bars; no note longer than two sixteenths. Riff E: 3+3+2+2 = 10 sixteenths, 6 times = 60, + two eighth notes placed after the fourth statement = 64 | Stengah (Nothing) | rose-2013 | R |
| Progressive and djent fusion | Meshuggah | 'Stengah' riffs A, H and I: 11 and 7 | Riff A is a motive in 11/8 that moves against the bar and is cut after 8 bars. Riffs H and I are in sevens and make the last statement longer to close the cycle. | Riff H: 7 sixteenths (3+4) x 9 = 63; the ninth statement becomes 3+5; total 64 = 4 bars; it sounds as eight bars of 7/16 and one bar of 2/4. Riff I: 3 phrases x 6 statements x 7 = 126; the last statement becomes 3+6; total 128 = 8 bars | Stengah (Nothing) | rose-2013 | R |
| Progressive and djent fusion | Meshuggah | 'Rational Gaze': 25/16 | The standard academic example (Pieslak 2007, reported by a later thesis). Guitars and bass play long odd bars; the drums play a slow 4/4 on top. | Guitars and bass: four bars of 25/16 + one bar of 28/16 = 128 sixteenths = 8 bars of 4/4; played twice = 16 bars in four groups of four. Cymbals: steady quarter notes. Snare: beat 3. Kick: with the guitars. The parts meet again at beat 64. 25/16 alone would need 16 statements to come back into line | Rational Gaze (Nothing) | eguez-2020; wiki-meshuggah | R |
| Progressive and djent fusion | Meshuggah | obZen riffs counted in quarter and eighth notes | Long riffs loop in a segment of 8 or 16 bars and are cut at its edge. A 'rotated' riff starts in the middle of its pattern. | 'Lethargica': riff of 23 quarter notes (5+6+6+6); 2 statements + 18 beats = 64 beats = 16 bars. 'Pravus': riff of 9 eighth notes with 15 attacks in an 8-bar segment; later a riff of 7 quarter notes (3+4) in 16 bars split 8+8, four full statements in each half | Lethargica; Pravus (obZen) | lucas-2018 | R |
| Progressive and djent fusion | Meshuggah | 'Pineal Gland Optics': pitch cycle against rhythm cycle | The rhythm pattern and the pitch pattern have different lengths, so each repeat joins new pitches to the rhythm. | Rhythm: 14 attacks in 12 eighth notes (6 quarter notes). Pitches: 8 notes. The two come into line after 56 attacks, at bars 7 and 13 of a 16-bar segment. The 8-bar verse has no fixed pattern: groups of 21+19+21+11+21+19+16 sixteenths = 128. Tempo quarter = 95 | Pineal Gland Optics (obZen) | lucas-2018 | R |
| Progressive and djent fusion | Meshuggah | 'Bleed' and 'Nostrum': more patterns | The 4/4 layer can move from one instrument to another, or drop away for a time. | 'Bleed': cymbals mark quarter notes for the first 1:25, then eighth notes; the kick plays the guitars' pattern of 3 sixteenths; at 1:40 the cymbals follow a pattern of 22 sixteenths and the voice carries the 4/4. 'Nostrum': durations 4, 2, 2, 3, 2, 3, 3 eighth notes = 19 eighths, plus a short tail to make 8 bars | Bleed (obZen); Nostrum | lucas-2018; mit-nostrum | R |
| Progressive and djent fusion | Meshuggah | Three ways to close a cycle | Named 'obliged re-phasing' in the analysis. The band chooses the way that makes the join least audible. | (1) Cut the last statement of the pattern. (2) Add a value: at the end of the cycle or at another place. (3) Make the last statement longer. Extra tools: an accent or a long note away from the start of the pattern; more accents than pitches; a changed first note on the repeat |  | rose-2013 | R |
| Progressive and djent fusion | Meshuggah | Drum layer over the riff | The drums hold two layers at once: the hands show the 4/4, the feet play the riff. Fast kick under a slow snare gives speed and slowness at the same time. | Cymbal or hi-hat on each quarter note. Snare on beat 3 of each bar. Kick doubles each attack of the guitar riff. A crash marks the start of each 4-bar cycle. Named the most common way Haake plays on a riff. Other snare roles: ornament; a 4:3 figure against the pulse ('I' at 5:40). To find the 4/4, follow the cymbals |  | rose-2013; pg-hagstrom-2017; lucas-2018 | R |
| Progressive and djent fusion | Animals as Leaders | 'Tempting Time': 19/16 over a quarter-note pulse | The band keeps a pulse that the listener can feel and puts all other parts in syncopation against it. | Riff in 19/16 played 4 times, then a turnaround of a dotted eighth and a sixteenth: 4 x 19 + 4 = 80 sixteenths = 5 bars of 4/4 (sum computed). Studio click: an eighth-note pulse with some accents; claps cue the parts | Tempting Time (2009) | gm-abasi-2; pg-aal-2014 | R |
| Progressive and djent fusion | Animals as Leaders | Groups of five | Abasi likes cells of five notes. They come from the hands: a tapping or thumping motion of five notes repeated in sixteenths or as quintuplets. | 'CAFO' tapping riff: fretting-hand groups of five notes with a tapped octave between. Thumping cell: 2 fretting-hand taps + thumb down + thumb up + finger pluck = 5. 'Gestaltzerfall': the fives became quintuplets 'in three'. The drummer also proposed accents over bars of 4/4 with one bar of 2/4 between them. Exercises: groups of five or three sixteenths that start off the beat | CAFO; Gestaltzerfall | gm-abasi-1; gw-abasi-lesson; mr-garstka-2022; vallejo-2020 | R |
| Progressive and djent fusion | Animals as Leaders | Bass drum technique | The drummer plays the kick with single strokes on two bass drums for power and adds short double strokes as detail. Blast beats are rare. | Fill-in figure 'one-e' (two sixteenths) on the kick. Blast beat sections on Parrhesia. Three songs have one short figure at extreme speed. The record has drum samples, but no samples replace the cymbals | Parrhesia | mr-garstka-2022 | R |
| Progressive and djent fusion | Tigran Hamasyan | 'Entertain Me': 35 sixteenths against 4/4 | A piano version of the Meshuggah method. A line of fives and threes repeats against a rock backbeat. | Line of 35 sixteenths = 5+5+5+5+3+3+3+3+3 (four bars of 5/16, five bars of 3/16). 7 statements (245) + one bar of 11/16 = 256 sixteenths = 16 bars of 4/4; then the section repeats. Drums: cymbal on each beat, snare on beat 3. Kick, bass and piano left hand play the first note of each group; the right hand plays all sixteenths | Entertain Me (Mockroot) | schumann-2021; eguez-2020; amarii-2025 | R |
| Progressive and djent fusion | Tigran Hamasyan | 'Vardavar': 32 sixteenths in uneven groups | An additive pattern that has the length of two bars of 4/4. Later the pattern is made shorter and set against a slower backbeat. | 5+5+3+5+5+4+5 = 32 sixteenths (piano, bass, kick) over a 4/4 backbeat (cymbals, snare). Later: 3+3+2+3+3+2.5+3.5 = 20 sixteenths over a slower 2/4 backbeat; both layers play twice before they meet | Vardavar (EP No. 1) | schumann-2021 | R |
| Progressive and djent fusion | Tigran Hamasyan | 'The Grid' and 'Out of the Grid' | One melody appears with groups of five and then with groups of four. | 'The Grid': 5+5+7+5+5+5 = 32 sixteenths. 'Out of the Grid': the same melody as 4+4+5+4+4+4 = 25 sixteenths over an ostinato in 5/16 (six melody bars = five ostinato bars); later over a 4/4 backbeat, until a step to a faster tempo breaks the cycle | The Grid; Out of the Grid (Mockroot) | schumann-2021 | R |
| Progressive and djent fusion | Tigran Hamasyan | 'Double-Faced': fives that fill 2 and 8 bars | Groups of five sixteenths with one longer group or a rest that completes the 4/4 cycle. | Intro: 5+5+5+5+5+7 = 32 sixteenths = 2 bars of 4/4; snare on beat 1. Section A: 6 groups of 5, four times = 120, + a rest of 8 = 128 = 8 bars; cymbals in half notes, snare on beat 3. Kick, bass and left hand mark the first note of each group | Double-Faced (Mockroot) | eguez-2020 | R |
| Progressive and djent fusion | Tigran Hamasyan | 'To Negate': 13/8 in half time | A motive in 13 against a slow rock beat. The second half keeps the motive and doubles the feel. | 13/8 = 3+3+3+2+2. Four bars of 13/8 (52 eighths) against five bars of 4/4 and one bar of 6/4. Cymbals in half notes, snare on beat 3, kick on the first eighth of each group. In section B the hi-hat plays sixteenths (normal time at quarter = 140) | To Negate (Mockroot) | eguez-2020 | R |
| Progressive and djent fusion | Tigran Hamasyan | Lines against a backbeat of another length | A melodic phrase and a drum pattern of different lengths run together until they meet, or until one part is cut. | 'A Crane Came From Van': 6+6+7 = 19 sixteenths against a 16-sixteenth backbeat; each statement starts 3 sixteenths later; after 4 statements the drums drop one beat. 'Song for Melan and Rafik': seven bars of 5/8 + one of 7/8 = 42 eighths; 2 phrases = 7 bars of a 12/8 backbeat; then a 4/8 pattern five times and a 3/8 pattern five times | A Crane Came From Van; Song for Melan and Rafik | schumann-2021 | R |
| Progressive and djent fusion | Tigran Hamasyan | 'New Maps': one ostinato, several groupings | The same ostinato can be heard in several metres. The high notes and the low notes group it in two ways. | Ostinato cycle of 40 eighth notes = 8 bars of 5/8. High notes: 6+7+5+4+6+7+5. Low notes: 6+6+6+4+6+6+6. The drums follow the melody but keep a quarter-note pulse; groups of four and eight bars | New Maps (The Call Within) | orco-2025 | R |
| Progressive and djent fusion | Tigran Hamasyan | Layers in different metres | Two or three metres run at the same time in one instrument or between piano and drums. | 'Etude No. 1': layers in 5/8 (2+3), 7/8 (3+4) and 3/8; the first two meet every 7 bars of 5/8; all three share a downbeat only once; layers are cut to keep 8-bar groups; 112 of 168 bars have cycles. 'Drip': a layer at the ratio 8:5 implies 5/4 against 4/4. 'Kars 1': written in 5/8 all through, but the cymbal marks make the riff passages sound in 4/4 | Etude No. 1; Drip; Kars 1 | schumann-2021; amarii-2025 | R |
| Progressive and djent fusion | Tigran Hamasyan | Size and sources of the cycle practice | Cycles are a regular device from the third album on. He names folk music, metal and Indian classical music as the sources of his rhythm. | 13 albums 2006 to 2020, 137 tracks, at least 39 cycle passages. Sources named: Armenian folk music, Tool, Meshuggah, Indian classical music. Practice routine: fives over elevens over a regular four-beat groove |  | schumann-2021; npr-tigran; bcd-tigran | R |
| Progressive and djent fusion | General | Konnakol syllables | South Indian spoken rhythm. Each group length has a fixed syllable set, so a long odd pattern can be spoken as a chain of sets. No source read here says that these artists use it; Hamasyan names Indian classical music, and John McLaughlin uses konnakol to compose. | 2 Tha Ka. 3 Tha Ki Ta. 4 Tha Ka Dhi Mi. 5 Tha Dhi Gi Na Thom. 6 Tha Ka Dhi Mi Tha Ka. 7 Tha Ka Di Mi Tha Ki Ta. 8 Tha Ka Dhi Mi Tha Ka Jho Nu. 9 Tha Ka Dhi Mi Ta Dhi Gi Na Thom |  | wiki-konnakol; npr-tigran | R |
| Progressive and djent fusion | General | Time feels | The feel depends on the rate of the kick-snare alternation, not on the tempo number. | Normal time: kick on beats 1 and 3, snare on 2 and 4. Half time: snare on beat 3. Double time: snare on the 'and' of each beat. Quarter time: kick and snare alternate bar by bar. Quadruple time: the traditional blast beat. Octuple time at 80 equals quadruple time at 160 |  | garza-2021 | R |
| Progressive and djent fusion | General | Breakdown riff | A low, palm-muted, syncopated riff in half time. Guitar, bass and often the kick drum play the uneven rhythm in unison, while a cymbal keeps a steady pulse. | Snare on beat 3. Crash or China cymbal in steady quarter notes. Kick doubles the guitar rhythm. In syncopated riffs of other types the guitar takes its rhythm from kick and snare; in a breakdown the guitar rhythm is the base |  | garza-2021; mit-tesseract | R |
| Progressive and djent fusion | General | Ways to group seven | Collected for a piece in 7. Most examples split seven as 3+4. | 7/16 as 3+4, nine statements in 4 bars of 4/4 ('Stengah'). 7 quarter notes as 3+4 ('Pravus'). 7/8 as 3+4 against 5/8 as 2+3 ('Etude No. 1'). One bar of 7/8 after seven bars of 5/8 ('Song for Melan and Rafik'). Konnakol: 4+3. Thumping exercises in groups of seven |  | rose-2013; lucas-2018; schumann-2021; wiki-konnakol; vallejo-2020 | R |
| Progressive and djent fusion | General | Metric modulation and tempo steps | A change of metre with one note value kept constant. In Hamasyan a cycle can also end with a step to a faster or slower tempo. | Eighth note constant: 5/8 with the bar at 90 a minute (450 eighths a minute) goes to 2/2 with the half note at 112.5 (example from a jazz quartet piece written with Meshuggah's methods). 'Out of the Grid': a step to a faster tempo breaks a cycle. 'Vardavar': the last section drops to a slower tempo for a new cycle |  | rose-2013; schumann-2021 | R |
| Progressive and djent fusion | General | Ghost notes and linear fills (estimate) | Standard drum practice of the fusion side of the style, not confirmed in a source read here. Soft snare notes fill the sixteenths between the accents. Fills are 'linear': no two limbs play at the same time. | Estimate: ghost notes at about one quarter of the loudness of the backbeat; linear fills in groups of 3, 5 or 7 sixteenths, 2 to 4 beats long, ending on a crash at the next downbeat |  | reference | K |
| Cosmic jazz | Hancock | Riff in 15/8 | A bar of 4/4 and a bar of 7/8. It comes from a syncopated 4/4 bass line with each second phrase made half a beat shorter. | 15/8 = 8 + 7 eighth notes. The riff runs for the full 13:10. Two drummers. Order of entries: bass clarinet, bass, congas and drums | Ostinato (Suite for Angela) | gluck-pod; wiki-mwandishi; iverson-mwandishi | R |
| Cosmic jazz | Hancock | Odd metres of the Mwandishi band | Funk grooves and ostinatos in long odd bars. | Hidden Shadows: groove in 19/4, A minor, cowbell. Quasar: ostinato in 7/4 on D-flat. Sleeping Giant: drum fanfare in 6/8 at the start | Sextant; Crossings | hh-sextant; iverson-mwandishi | R |
| Cosmic jazz | Hancock | Bass line of 12 notes | A line of 12 notes in 2 bars. Chromatic steps lead up to the root of each chord. | Roots B-flat and E-flat on beat 1 of each bar. The arpeggio notes of the line fall every 3 sixteenth notes (tresillo). Chromatic walk-up notes come before them; approach notes A and D are outside the mode. Vamp: B-flat m7 and E-flat 7 | Chameleon | wiki-chameleon; hein-chameleon | R |
| Cosmic jazz | Hancock | Swing of the sixteenth notes | The bass line alone is in straight sixteenth notes. From the entry of the drums, bass and drums swing the sixteenth notes by amounts that change. | Drums enter at bar 5. The analyst tests a constant swing of 66% for the sixteenth notes. Hi-hat late; some snare notes early. The tempo goes up slowly during the track | Chameleon | hein-dilla | R |
| Cosmic jazz | Hancock | Actual Proof: form and drums | Bars of 5, 4, and 3 beats end the theme. The drummer does not mark the start of the form. | Vamp 16 bars; A 13 bars (last three bars: 5/4, 4/4, 3/4); vamp 4 bars; solo on A and 2 vamp bars, 15 times; vamp 8 bars; last solo over 34 vamp bars. Backbeat moved or left out; fills in sixteenth-note triplets; one take | Actual Proof | torres-clark; jj-clark; ser-clark | R |
| Cosmic jazz | Hancock | Palm Grease: form | A funk form with mixed bar lengths in one section. | Vamp 8 bars; A 24; A1 9; B 17 bars that alternate 6/4 and 4/4, with the last bar in 2/4; C 16; D open solo until a cue; then from A again with no C; fade. The hi-hat opens on upbeats in some sections and on downbeats in others | Palm Grease | torres-clark | R |
| Cosmic jazz | Hancock | Sly: drums on the theme | The drums play the theme in unison with the band. Then the feel changes to a fast samba-like groove for the solos. | "faux-samba" (Pierard). The percussion fills the gaps that the drums leave. 10:15. Database tempo: 146 BPM | Sly | pierard-hh; wiki-headhunters; songbpm-cosmic | R |
| Cosmic jazz | Mahavishnu Orchestra | Three groupings of 18 | One bar of 18 eighth notes. Each instrument groups it in a different way. | 18/8. Guitar: 5+5+5+3. Drums: 6+6+6. Violin (at times): 3+3+2+3+3+2+2 | Birds of Fire | wiki-oddmetres | R |
| Cosmic jazz | Mahavishnu Orchestra | Bar of 19 | A riff of 19 eighth notes. The drums count it in a different way from the bass. | 19/8 (also listed as 19/16). Riff and bass: 3/4 + 3/4 + 7/8. Drums: a half-time 4/4 funk beat plus 3 (16 + 3). Form of 11 bars; in the theme bar 7 has 18. The soloist follows the drums | Celestial Terrestrial Commuters | mertanen2025; wiki-oddmetres | R |
| Cosmic jazz | Mahavishnu Orchestra | Other metres | Bars of 7, 9, 11, 15, 17, 19, and 20. | Vital Transformation: drum break in 9/8 at the start. The Dance of Maya (solos): 20 as 3+3+3+3+3+3+2, with displaced accents. Dream: 15 as 3+3+3+3+3 or 2+2+2+2+2+2+3. The bass player in 1973: "7's, 9's, 11's, 17's, 19's" | The Inner Mounting Flame; Between Nothingness & Eternity | jazzimp-vital; keith-meters; pg-mclaughlin; progro-bof | R |
| Cosmic jazz | Mahavishnu Orchestra | Meeting of the Spirits: metre | The usual count of the main riff. | 12/8 (or 6/8), with the arpeggio in groups of 5 + 7 notes. Not confirmed in a source during this study | Meeting of the Spirits | reference | K |
| Cosmic jazz | Weather Report | Funk in three | A groove in 3/4 with a second layer in 4/4. | Snare on each beat; bass drum on the "and" before beat 1 and on beat 1; clay drums play 4/4 over the 3/4 (3 against 4); 13:01. Zawinul: "a hip-hop in 3" | Boogie Woogie Waltz | wax-sweetnighter; wr-bianchi-sweet | R |
| Cosmic jazz | Weather Report | One drum pattern for a full piece | The drums keep one pattern. The other parts change. | Hi-hat in eighth notes; snare rim on each quarter note; bass-drum figure of 2 bars; tambourine. On stage from 1978: a shuffle beat | Birdland | engel-birdland; wr-bianchi-830 | R |
| Cosmic jazz | Weather Report | Two drummers, two feels | Two drummers play different feels, together or one after the other. | Mysterious Traveller: swing beat against straight rock and funk beat, recorded apart and then combined. Black Market: straight eighth notes, then swing after a hard tape edit to the second drummer. Herandnu: 11/4 | Mysterious Traveller; Black Market | wr-bianchi-myst; wr-bianchi-black | R |
| Cosmic jazz | Sun Ra | Layers of rhythm | Two to four rhythm layers go at the same time. The band can also play with no steady beat. | Sun Ra: 2, 3, or 4 rhythm layers at once. Piece of 1968: lightning drum, log drum, and more percussion in asymmetric cross-rhythms. A free-tempo groove that enters and exits on cue | Continuation; Heliocentric Worlds Vol. 3 | gagne-sunra; coleman-srs | R |
| Beat scene, IDM, dubstep | J Dilla | Three time feels (Charnas) | Straight time: all subdivisions are even. Swing time: the two halves of each beat are uneven. Dilla time: straight layers and swung layers sound together, and some parts are before or after the grid. It is not a midpoint between straight and swing. | "straight and swung at the same time" | Book Dilla Time (2022) | hein-dilla-time; charnas-djmag; charnas-okp; wiki-dilla-time | R |
| Beat scene, IDM, dubstep | J Dilla | Early snare against straight hi-hats | The hi-hats are exactly on the grid. The snare comes early. The ear hears swing that is not there. | Charnas saw this when he put the waveform on the grid of an audio workstation | Chino XL, Don't Say a Word (beat of about 1998) | charnas-djmag; charnas-uproxx | R |
| Beat scene, IDM, dubstep | J Dilla | Swing for each sound, plus shift | The SP-1200 has one swing value for all sounds. The MPC lets each sound have its own swing, for example swung hi-hats with a straight kick. The MPC can also shift a part earlier or later. The result is a grid that is out of line in the same way in each loop. | Dilla got the MPC3000 in 1997 or 1998; the feel is more than "quantise off" | Method | charnas-djmag; musicradar-dilla | R |
| Beat scene, IDM, dubstep | J Dilla | Timing values of the MPC3000 | The Timing Correct page sets the note value, the swing and the shift. | Note values: 1/8, 1/8 triplet, 1/16, 1/16 triplet, 1/32, 1/32 triplet, off. Swing: 50 to 75 percent for 1/8 or 1/16 notes; 50 = even; 66 = "a technically perfect swing". Shift: earlier or later in ticks; 1 tick = 1/96 of a quarter note = about 7 ms (6.9 ms at 90 BPM, computed) | MPC3000 | mpc3000-manual; simonelli-substack | R |
| Beat scene, IDM, dubstep | Roger Linn | Swing settings for 16th notes | The designer of the MPC gives these values. | 50 percent: no swing. 54: loosens straight 16ths and does not sound like swing. 62 at 90 BPM: looser than the exact 66. Useful range: 50 to about 70. Delay of the off 16th at 90 BPM (computed): 54 = 13 ms; 58 = 27 ms; 62 = 40 ms; 66.7 = 56 ms | Drum machines | linn-brettworks | R |
| Beat scene, IDM, dubstep | J Dilla | Size and direction of the offsets | Writers give the size as tens of milliseconds or less. The same offset repeats in each loop, and the repeat makes it strong. The direction changes from track to track: one account has the kick early, the snare late and the hi-hat on the grid; other accounts have the snare early. | "a kick hits 30ms early here, a snare drags by 20ms there"; Dilla called his units "baby hairs" | Method | musicradar-dilla; frerejones-4col; hein-dilla-time; simonelli-substack | R |
| Beat scene, IDM, dubstep | J Dilla | Analyses of four tracks | Each track has its own map of early and late parts. | "Get Dis Money": near-straight eighths; backbeat claps a little early; some hi-hats late; no kick on the downbeat. "Say It": on-beat hi-hats straight; off-beat hi-hats and kicks late, but less than a triplet; snares mostly early. "U-Love": hi-hats and snares late; kicks on the grid except the delayed 16ths. "Fall in Love": synth and hi-hat swung and shifted; kick and snare straight | Four tracks | hein-dilla-time; soundfly-dilla; pudding-groove; musicradar-dilla | R |
| Beat scene, IDM, dubstep | Dilla feel | Swing as tuplets and as percent | Divide the beat into an odd number of parts. Put the off-beat on a late part. | Triplet 2:1 = 66.7 percent; quintuplet 3:2 = 60; septuplet 4:3 = 57.1; nonuplet 5:4 = 55.6; ratio 5:3 = 62.5 (off-beat one 32nd note late; near the golden ratio at 61.8). Example of 5:3: Kendrick Lamar, "How Much a Dollar Cost" | Analysis of the style | pudding-groove; brl-dilla2; brl-dilla3; pocketchops | R |
| Beat scene, IDM, dubstep | Dilla feel | Recipes with two subdivisions at once | The parts agree on the beat and rub against each other inside the beat (flams). | (a) Hi-hat in septuplet swing and kick one 32nd note after the straight eighth; triplet melody above. (b) Straight kick, hi-hat between straight and septuplet swing, snare and hi-hat a little early on the backbeat (Dilla, "Kamaal"). (c) Kick stutters on 32nd notes or on septuplets. (d) Snare a 64th note early on beat 2 (42 ms at 90 BPM, computed). (e) Drift: hi-hat before septuplet swing in bar 1 and after it in bar 4 of a 4-bar loop | Analysis of the style | brl-dilla2; brl-dilla3 | R |

Reported faults: an automatic update during a soundcheck; one extra beat added in a song in 7/8. | Progressive and djent fusion | General (Misha Mansoor, Periphery) | Recipe for the tight rhythm tone | The 'djent' tone is a boost pedal in front of a high-gain amp with less gain than a player expects, and noise gates that make the rig silent between the notes. | (1) As little amp gain as possible: stop when palm mutes start to sustain. (2) Amp EQ at 12 o'clock, then mids a little down and highs up. (3) Overdrive pedal before the amp: drive at or near zero, level high; it tightens the low end. (4) Noise gates on the pedal and on the amp. (5) Tape or a damper over the strings at the nut |  | sw-mansoor | R |

| Progressive and djent fusion | General | Kick and snare sound | The kick is reinforced or replaced by samples for a machine-like, even attack, and it is built from layers. High-pass the kick at 30 to 40 Hz with 18 to 24 dB an octave; cut 200 to 500 Hz. Beater click in a dense mix: 3 to 5 kHz. Snare: body 200 to 250 Hz, crack 900 Hz to 1 kHz, wires 5 kHz. Cymbals: 6 to 10 kHz |  | ntm-kick; ntm-eq-chart; mr-garstka-2022 | R |

Grit track: high-pass at 200 to 300 Hz before the distortion, boost at 1.5 to 4 kHz for the pick 'clank', level 6 to 10 dB below the low track. Bass weight at 80 to 120 Hz with the kick at 60 Hz; growl at 400 to 800 Hz. Meshuggah: pick and overdrive pedals. Animals as Leaders: no recording together in one room; the drummer plays to the finished guitars. Meshuggah 2016: guitar, bass, drums and voice recorded together, no edits. | Progressive and djent fusion | Thall bands | Thall guitar tone | A dark, clicking tone from an old amp model, a mid boost and heavy EQ after a dark cabinet response. | Amp model 'Big Bottom' of a Line 6 Pod, named as the guitar sound of Meshuggah's Catch Thirtythree: gain 50%, bass 15%, mids 85%, treble 60%, presence 70% (a second source says: gain just past the halfway point, bass rolled off, mids and treble high). Boost before the amp: mid frequency 51%, treble 60%, bass 60%; it adds bass and upper mids and does not cut lows as a Tube Screamer does. Compressor: slow attack, fast release. Very dark cabinet impulse response, then a large EQ; mid boost on the mid channel only |  | gw-thall; ntm-vildhjarta | R |
| Progressive and djent fusion | Tosin Abasi | Rhythm, lead and thump tones | Abasi blends two amp models: one modern and tight in the low end, one vintage with less gain and more mids. Thump tone: gate with the shortest attack; compressor; soft-crunch Marshall type + Fender Bassman type; controls at 12 o'clock, treble a little higher; cut near 500 Hz; single-coil or coil-split pickup. Later: real EL34 high-gain head with a noise gate, in stereo |  | mr-aal-axe-fx; pg-aal-2013; vallejo-2020; gw-bad-cat | R |

| Progressive and djent fusion | Meshuggah | Amps and clarity of the low string | Very high gain, with the whole chain built to control it. The clarity of the lowest string comes from the pick attack, the long scale and a pickup that adds no power. | About 1999: Mesa/Boogie Dual Rectifier in the studio, wax-potted pickups, a noise gate in the effects loop. 2016: a modified Marshall, a 120 W head and a 50 W signature head for rhythm; Axe-Fx only for solos and cleans; a boost pedal with a sweepable bass filter. Scale 27 to 29.4 inches. For very low tunings also: high string action. The eighth string needs its own pick attack; the pick angle changes the tone. Down strokes alone give the hardest attack (Abasi rarely uses them) |  | gw-meshuggah-sound; pg-hagstrom-2017; mixdown-meshuggah; ntm-vildhjarta; gw-abasi-plini | R |

### S12. Devices

| Circle | Artist or study | Item | Content | Values | Where | Source | Code |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Sungazer | Sungazer | Two ways to hear one pattern | Neely compares the change of feel in Cool 7 with a Necker cube: the same pattern has two readings. Two processes work together: a pulse morph and a metric modulation. He names Tigran Hamasyan and Malcolm Braff as earlier users; another analyst calls the device 'shifting gravity centers'. The source may be aksak rhythm. | 2 readings of one 14-eighth cycle: 4/4 with a short beat, or 7/4 | Cool 7 | neely-cool7 | R |
| Sungazer | Sungazer | The audience as a rhythm instrument | Before a hard passage the audience gets a two-step dance or a clap pattern. Steady movement lets them feel the change of pulse in the body. Fans call for odd time signatures at the end of concerts. | Dance: 2 steps right, 2 steps left. Clap pattern in 7/8. Count games: '1, 2, 1 2 3 4 5'; counting from five to seven with no six | Concerts 2022 to 2025 | neely-cool7; emr-glasgow25; wrvu-live22; ha-interview; postmusic-live25 | R |
| Sungazer | Sungazer | Breakdown by slower subdivision | The breakdown keeps the pulse and makes the subdivision slower for impact. A reviewer hears djent-style breakdowns in concert. | 11 notes per beat to 7 notes per beat | Against the Fall of Night (title track); concerts | neely-44; postmusic-live25 | R |
| Sungazer | Sungazer | Slowest pulse with fastest notes | Threshold puts notes at the fast limit of rhythm on a very slow pulse. | Progressive and djent fusion | Tosin Abasi | Thumping | A slap-bass technique moved to the guitar. | Progressive and djent fusion | Tosin Abasi | Selective picking, hybrid picking, 'swybrid' | Selective picking is between alternate picking and legato: some notes are picked, the others are hammered by the fretting hand with no pick stroke. Hybrid picking adds the fingers. 'Swybrid' joins sweep picking and finger plucks. | Basic cell in sixteenths: two picked notes, two hammered notes. | Progressive and djent fusion | Tosin Abasi; Plini | Tapping | The picking hand taps a note one octave above the fretted note, or adds the top note of an arpeggio. Tapped arpeggio: four notes on two strings plus a tapped note at fret 12. Mute the other strings with a light barre and the side of the picking hand. | Feel and lines | McCoy Tyner; Miles Davis and Bill Evans | Fourths in chords and lines | The 'So What' chord has three perfect fourths and a major third on top. | Feel and lines | John Coltrane | Sheets of sound: three chords over one | Coltrane put three chords over one: over C7 he added E-flat 7 and F-sharp 7. | Feel and lines | Drum practice | Ghost notes | A ghost note is a snare stroke at very low volume. Drummers play ghost notes on the off-beat sixteenth notes between the main notes. They give a soft sixteenth-note feel under a strong backbeat. | Position: off-beat 16ths; level: see the measured ratio 0.28 | 'Cold Sweat' (Stubblefield); 'Rosanna' (Porcaro); 'Amen, Brother' | wiki-ghost | R |
| Feel and lines | Reference knowledge | Enclosures and approach notes | An enclosure puts a target chord tone between an upper and a lower neighbour: usually a scale step above and a semitone below. It has two to four notes and the target is on a beat. A single approach note is usually a semitone below the target. | Enclosure: +1 or +2, then -1, then the target on the beat | Not confirmed in a source in this task | reference | K |
| Feel and lines | Reference knowledge | Triad pairs, diminished and whole-tone material | A triad pair uses two triads with no common tone, usually a whole step apart, in cells of three notes. The half-whole diminished scale is used over dominant 7 flat 9 chords. The whole-tone scale is used over dominant 7 sharp 5 chords. | Triad pair: 6 notes in 3-note cells; diminished scale: 8 notes | Not confirmed in a source in this task | reference | K |

It is usually longer than an improvised phrase, has fewer rests, and uses sequences and rhythmic hits with the drums. The players align their onsets closely. | No measured values found | Not confirmed in a source in this task | reference | K |

## Part 3: Style profiles

A style profile is a set of target ranges for the new work. Each range has a low value and a high value, around a measured value or a sourced value. The column "Basis" gives that value. The design files and the code of the work use these ranges.

### P1. Profile "All": the full work

| Form | Sections (one change of the music) | 20 | 26 | sections | Whalefall: about 11 events in 5:46 (one change each 31 s) | R |
| Form | Time at the full level | 0.53 | 0.77 | share of the duration | Six reference tracks: 0.53 to 0.77 | M |
| Form | First full level | 0.03 | 0.16 | share of the duration | Six reference tracks: 11 to 49 s of 205 to 346 s | M |

## Limits of the study

- The study measured six of the nine tracks of one album. The model track "Cool 7" was not available.
- The stems come from a separation program. A stem has some sound of the other instruments.
- The drum strokes come from rules for the sound of each drum. The check pictures show that the rules find the loud strokes. Soft strokes and tom-toms are less certain.
- The timing spread has the error of the measurement (about 3 to 5 ms).
- The notes of the stem "other" come from a transcription model. Chords count as some note starts at one time: the values are not the speed of single lines.
- Tempos and keys in the sourced tables are frequently values of databases, made by algorithms. The rows say this.
- The research tasks had a limit for web searches. Some sources were not available. Each task gave a list of the items that it could not verify: the rows with the code K have such items.
- The rows with the code K are not checked.
- The composer of the code cannot hear audio. The checks of the sound are measurements and the judgement of the owner.
